                   // version 4\n                    break;\n                case 19:\n                    uuid[i] = hex[(randomByte() & 0x3) | 0x8]; // variant\n                    break;\n                default:\n                    if (bits < 4)\n                    {\n                        rnd  = randomByte();\n                        bits = 8;\n                    }\n                    uuid[i] = hex[(rnd >> (bits -= 4)) & 0xf];\n            }\n        }\n        return uuid;\n    }\n\n    static int randomByte()\n    {\n        // Thread-local pseudo-random byte.  In production use <random> or\n        // OS-supplied CSPRNG for cryptographic quality.\n        thread_local uint32_t state =\n            static_cast<uint32_t>(std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now()\n                                      .time_since_epoch()\n                                      .count());\n        state = state * 1664525u + 1013904223u;\n        return static_cast<int>((state >> 24) & 0xFF);\n    }\n\n    // ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n    // Observer helpers\n    // ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n    void notifyObservers(const Result& r)\n    {\n        for (auto& obs : observers_) {\n            if (obs) { obs(*this, r); }\n        }\n    }\n\nprivate:\n    ScanContext                             context_;\n    std::shared_ptr<infra::IEventBus>       bus_;\n    std::vector<Observer>                   observers_;\n};\n\n} // namespace fortiledger360::domain::commands\n\n#endif  // FORTILEDGER360_DOMAIN_COMMANDS_SCAN_COMMAND_H","numLines":333,"startLine":1,"totalLines":333}}}

/*-------------------------------------------------------------------*/\n    static std::string generateCorrelationId()\n    {\n        thread_local std::mt19937_64 rng{std::random_device{}()};\n std::priority_queue<QueueItem> newQueue;\n        bool found = false;\n\n        while (!pending_.empty())\n        {\n            auto item = pending_.top();\n            pending_.pop();\n\n            if (item.correlationId == correlationId)\n            {\n                /* Create immediate cancellation result. */\n                ScanResult result;\n                result.correlationId = ScanState::Cancelled;\n                result.errorMessage  = \"Scan cancelled before execution.\";\n                result.duration      = std::chrono::milliseconds{0};\n\n                {\n });\n}\n\nvoid ContinuousScanStrategy::stop() {\n    {\n        std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lk(_lifecycleMtx);\n        if (!_running.exchange(false)) {\n            return;  // not running\n        }\n    }\n\n    if (_worker.joinable()) {\n        _worker.join();\n    }\n\n    spdlog::info(\"[ContinuousScanStrategy] Stopped scanning loop for tenant \"\n                 \"'{}'\",\n                 _tenantId);\n}\n\nvoid ContinuousScanStrategy::run() {\n    auto nextSleep = _scanInterval;\n    // Randomize first iteration to avoid thundering herd\n    nextSleep += _calculateJitter();\n\n    while (_running.load()) {\n        std::this_thread::sleep_for(nextSleep);\n        if (!_running.load()) break;\n\n        try {\n            auto allowed =\n                _policyEvaluator->isScanAllowed(_tenantId, std::chrono::system_clock::now());\n            if (!allowed) {\n                spdlog::warn(\n                    \"[ContinuousScanStrategy] Scan skipped (policy violation) \"\n                    \"for tenant '{}'\",\n                    _tenantId);\n                publishPolicyViolation();\n                // Exponential back-off on policy violation\n                nextSleep = std::min(nextSleep * 2, 10 * _scanInterval);\n                continue;\n            }\n\n            performScan();\n            nextSleep = _scanInterval + _calculateJitter(); Issue scan request and collect results.\n    auto scanId = _scannerClient->initiateScan(_tenantId, assets);\n    publishScanStarted(scanId, assets.size());\n\n    auto results = _scannerClient->awaitScanResults(scanId);\n\n    // 3. Publish results to interested parties (billing, monitoring, etc.)\n    publishScanCompleted(scanId, results);\n\n    // 4. House-keeping: enforce post-scan hooks (e.g., billing metering)\n    _scannerClient->postScanFinalize(_tenantId, scanId);\n}\n\n//\n// Domain Event helpers\n//\nvoid ContinuousScanStrategy::publishScanStarted(const std::string& scanId,\n                                                std::size_t assetCount) {\n    events::ScanStarted evt{_tenantId, scanId, assetCount};\n    _eventBus->publish(evt);\n}\n\nvoid ContinuousScanStrategy::publishScanCompleted(\n    const std::string& scanId, const infra::ScanResults& results) {\n    events::ScanCompleted evt{_tenantId, scanId, results};\n    _eventBus->publish(evt);\n}\n\nvoid ContinuousScanStrategy::publishScanFailure(const std::string& reason) {\n    events::ScanFailed evt{_tenantId, reason};\n    _eventBus->publish(evt);\n}\n\nvoid ContinuousScanStrategy::publishPolicyViolation() {\n    events::ScanPolicyViolation evt{_tenantId};\n    _eventBus->publish(evt);\n}\n\n//\n// Utility\n//\nstd::chrono::seconds ContinuousScanStrategy::_calculateJitter() const {\n    if (_jitter.count() == 0) return 0s;\n\n    static thread_local std::mt19937 rng{\n        static_cast<unsigned>(std::chrono::steady_clock::now().time_since_epoch().count())};\n    std::uniform_int_distribution<int> dist(0, static_cast<int>(_jitter.count()));\n    return std::chrono::seconds(dist(rng));\n}\n\n}  // namespace fortiledger360::domain::strategies","numLines":192,"startLine":1,"totalLines":192}}}

task.topic, task.qos);\n    }\n\n    void _handleSendFailure(const Task& task, const std::exception& ex)\n    {\n        // Basic exponential backoff w/ bounded retries\n        static constexpr int maxRetries   = 5;\n        static constexpr auto baseBackoff = std::chrono::milliseconds(100);\n\n        for (int attempt = 1; attempt <= maxRetries && _running.load(); ++attempt)\n        {\n serializer_.serialize(evt);\n    task.timeout    = defaultTimeout_;\n    task.promise    = std::make_shared<std::promise<void>>();\n\n    {\n        std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lg(queueMtx_);\n        queue_.emplace_back(std::move(task));\n    }\n    queueCv_.notify_one();\n\n    return task.promise->get_future();\n}\n\nvoid EventPublisher::Impl::flush()\n{\n    std::unique_lock<std::mutex> std::unique_lock<std::mutex> ul(queueMtx_);\n            queueCv_.wait(ul, [this] { return !queue_.empty() || !running_; });\n\n            if (!running_ && queue_.empty())\n                break;\n\n cap      = 2s;\n    auto delay          = base * (1ULL << attempt);\n    if (delay > cap) delay = cap;\n\n    std::this_thread::sleep_for(delay);\n}\n\n/* ----------  EventPublisher public façade This decouples the infrastructure plumbing from domain logic\n *  and facilitates unit-testing of business workflows without a running broker.\n *\n **************************************************************************************************/\n\n#include <atomic>\n#include <chrono>\n#include <cstdint>\n#include <functional>\n#include <memory>\n#include <mutex>\n#include <string>\n#include <string_view>\n#include <unordered_map>\n#include <utility>\n\n#include <nlohmann/json.hpp>      // https://github.com/nlohmann/json\n\n// Forward declaration of the broker connection. static_assert(std::is_default_constructible_v<TEvent>,\n                  \"Subscribed event types must be default-constructible.\");\n\n    const std::string key{getEventKey<TEvent>()};\n\n    auto dispatcher = std::make_shared<Dispatcher<TEvent>>(std::move(handler));\n\n    {\n        std::lock_guard lk{_mutex};\n        if (_dispatchers.contains(key))\n        {\n            throw connection)\n    : _connection(std::move(connection))\n{\n    if (!_connection)\n    {\n        throw std::invalid_argument(\"IBusConnection must not be null\");\n    }\n}\n\ninline EventConsumer::start()\n{\n    bool expected = false;\n    if (_running.compare_exchange_strong(expected, true, std::memory_order_acq_rel))\n    {\n        // Hook to the connection.  Each implementation of IBusConnection must offer a mechanism\n        // to supply a callback executed on the calling thread or on its own IO context.\n        // Example pseudo-code:\n        //\n        //     _connection->setMessageCallback([self = shared_from_this()](auto&& raw) {\n        //         self->pushRawMessage(raw);\n        //     });\n        //\n        // Not implemented here; queueCond.notify_one();\n        }\n        catch (const cppkafka::HandleException& ex) {\n            log_->error(\"Kafka exception: {}\", ex.what());\n        }\n        catch (const std::exception& ex) {\n            log_->error(\"Unexpected exception: {}\", ex.what());\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Signal processing threads to finish\n    {\n        std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(queueMutex);\n  std::string  tenantId;\n    std::string  resourceId;\n    std::size_t  findings;        // Number of detected issues\n    std::chrono::milliseconds duration;\n    bool         success;\n    std::string  failureReason;\n};\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Observer Pattern\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\nenum class EngineEvent\n{\n    kScanStarted,\n    kScanFinished,\n    kScanFailed\n};\n\nclass IEngineObserver\n{\npublic:\n    virtual ~IEngineObserver()                                      = default;\n    virtual void onEvent(EngineEvent ev, const ScanCommand &cmd,\n                         const std::optional<ScanResult> &res) = 0;\n};\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Strategy Pattern\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\nclass IScanStrategy\n{\npublic:\n    virtual ~IScanStrategy() = default;\n    virtual ScanResult execute(const ScanCommand &cmd) = 0;\n};\n\nclass LightScanStrategy final : public IScanStrategy\n{\npublic:\n    explicit LightScanStrategy(Logger &log) : log_(log) {}\n\n    ScanResult execute(const ScanCommand &cmd) override\n    {\n        log_.log(LogLevel::kDebug, \"[LightScan] Tenant=\", cmd.tenantId, \" Resource=\", cmd.resourceId);\n        auto begin = std::chrono::steady_clock::now();\n\n        // Simulate a quick port scan & config diff\n        std::this_thread::sleep_for(std::chrono::milliseconds(250 + rand() % 150));\n\n        ScanResult res;\n        res.tenantId  = cmd.tenantId;\n        res.resourceId= cmd.resourceId;\n        res.findings  = rand() % 3; // Few findings expected\n        res.success   = true;\n        res.duration  = std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::milliseconds>(\n            std::chrono::steady_clock::now() - begin);\n        return res;\n    }\n\nprivate:\n    Logger &log_;\n};\n\nclass DeepScanStrategy final : public IScanStrategy\n{\npublic:\n    explicit DeepScanStrategy(Logger &log) : log_(log) {}\n\n    ScanResult execute(const ScanCommand &cmd) override\n    {\n        log_.log(LogLevel::kDebug, \"[DeepScan] Tenant=\", cmd.tenantId, \" Resource=\", cmd.resourceId);\n        auto begin = std::chrono::steady_clock::now();\n\n        // Simulate heavy CVE DB lookup, full file system scan, etc.\n        std::this_thread::sleep_for(std::chrono::seconds(1 + rand() % 2));\n\n        ScanResult res;\n        res.tenantId  = cmd.tenantId;\n        res.resourceId= cmd.resourceId;\n        res.findings  = 5 + rand() % 42;\n        res.success   = true;\n        res.duration  = std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::milliseconds>(\n            std::chrono::steady_clock::now() - begin);\n        return res;\n    }\n\nprivate:\n    Logger &log_;\n};\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Thread-safe Queue\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\ntemplate <typename T>\nclass BlockingQueue\n{\npublic:\n    explicit BlockingQueue(std::size_t max) : capacity_(max) {}\n\n    void push(T item)\n    {\n        std::unique_lock lk(mu_);\n        cvFull_.wait(lk, [&] { return queue_.size() < capacity_; });\n        queue_.push(std::move(item));\n        cvEmpty_.notify_one();\n    }\n\n    bool pop(T &out)\n    {\n        std::unique_lock lk(mu_);\n        cvEmpty_.wait(lk, [&] { return !queue_.empty() || terminated_; });\n        if (queue_.empty()) return false; // terminated\n        out = std::move(queue_.front());\n        queue_.pop();\n        cvFull_.notify_one();\n        return true;\n    }\n\n    void terminate()\n    {\n        {\n            std::lock_guard lg(mu_);\n            terminated_ = true;\n        }\n        cvEmpty_.notify_all();\n    }\n\nprivate:\n    std::size_t                capacity_;\n    std::queue<T>              queue_;\n    std::mutex                 mu_;\n    std::condition_variable    cvFull_, cvEmpty_;\n    bool                       terminated_ {false};\n};\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Scanner Engine (Command, Strategy, Observer, Concurrency)\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\nclass ScannerEngine\n{\npublic:\n    explicit ScannerEngine(std::size_t workerCount = std::thread::hardware_concurrency())\n        : queue_(1024), log_(), shuttingDown_(false)\n    {\n        workerCount = std::max<std::size_t>(1, workerCount);\n        log_.log(LogLevel::kInfo, \"Initializing ScannerEngine with \", workerCount, \" workers.\");\n\n        for (std::size_t i = 0; i < workerCount; ++i)\n        {\n            workers_.emplace_back([this, i] { workerLoop(i); });\n        }\n    }\n\n    ~ScannerEngine()\n    {\n        shutdown();\n    }\n\n    // Non-copyable\n    ScannerEngine(const ScannerEngine &)            = delete;\n    ScannerEngine &operator=(const ScannerEngine &) = delete;\n\n    void submit(const ScanCommand &cmd)\n    {\n        if (shuttingDown_.load()) throw std::runtime_error(\"Engine is shutting down\");\n        queue_.push(cmd);\n    }\n\n    void registerObserver(std::shared_ptr<IEngineObserver> obs)\n    {\n        if (!obs) return;\n        std::lock_guard lk(obsMu_);\n        observers_.push_back(std::move(obs));\n    }\n\n    void shutdown()\n    {\n        bool expected = false;\n        if (!shuttingDown_.compare_exchange_strong(expected, true))\n            return; // already called\n\n        log_.log(LogLevel::kInfo, \"ScannerEngine shutting down...\");\n        queue_.terminate();\n\n        for (auto &t : workers_)\n            if (t.joinable()) t.join();\n\n        log_.log(LogLevel::kInfo, \"All worker threads joined.\");\n    }\n\nprivate:\n    void workerLoop(std::size_t idx)\n    {\n        try\n        {\n            while (true)\n            {\n                ScanCommand cmd;\n                if (!queue_.pop(cmd)) break; // queue terminated\n\n                notify(EngineEvent::kScanStarted, cmd, std::nullopt);\n\n                auto strategy = makeStrategy(cmd.intensity);\n\n                ScanResult res;\n                try\n                {\n                    res = strategy->execute(cmd);\n                    notify(EngineEvent::kScanFinished, cmd, res);\n                }\n                catch (const std::exception &ex)\n                {\n                    res.success       = false;\n                    res.failureReason = ex.what();\n                    notify(EngineEvent::kScanFailed, cmd, res);\n                    log_.log(LogLevel::kError, \"Worker#\", idx, \" failed: \", ex.what());\n                }\n            }\n        }\n        catch (const std::exception &e)\n        {\n            log_.log(LogLevel::kError, \"Critical error in worker#\", idx, \": \", e.what());\n        }\n    }\n\n    std::unique_ptr<IScanStrategy> makeStrategy(ScanIntensity in)\n    {\n        switch (in)\n        {\n        case ScanIntensity::kLight: return std::make_unique<LightScanStrategy>(log_);\n        case ScanIntensity::kDeep:  return std::make_unique<DeepScanStrategy>(log_);\n        }\n        throw std::invalid_argument(\"Unknown intensity\");\n    }\n\n    void notify(EngineEvent ev, const ScanCommand &cmd,\n                const std::optional<ScanResult> &res)\n    {\n        std::vector<std::shared_ptr<IEngineObserver>> snapshot;\n        {\n            std::lock_guard lg(obsMu_);\n            snapshot = observers_;\n        }\n        for (auto &o : snapshot)\n        {\n            try\n            {\n                o->onEvent(ev, cmd, res);\n            }\n            catch (const std::exception &e)\n            {\n                log_.log(LogLevel::kWarn, \"Observer threw: \", e.what());\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\nprivate:\n    BlockingQueue<ScanCommand>                  queue_;\n    std::vector<std::thread>                    workers_;\n    std::vector<std::shared_ptr<IEngineObserver>> observers_;\n    std::mutex                                  obsMu_;\n    Logger                                      log_;\n    std::atomic_bool                            shuttingDown_;\n};\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Sample Observer(s) to demonstrate behaviour\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\nclass ConsoleObserver final : public IEngineObserver\n{\npublic:\n    explicit ConsoleObserver(Logger &log) : log_(log) {}\n\n    void onEvent(EngineEvent ev, const ScanCommand &cmd,\n                 const std::optional<ScanResult> &res) override\n    {\n        switch (ev)\n        {\n        case EngineEvent::kScanStarted:\n            log_.log(LogLevel::kInfo, \"[Observer] Scan started for \", cmd.resourceId);\n            break;\n        case EngineEvent::kScanFinished:\n            log_.log(LogLevel::kInfo, \"[Observer] Scan finished (\", res->findings,\n                     \" findings, took \", res->duration.count(), \"ms)\");\n            break;\n        case EngineEvent::kScanFailed:\n            log_.log(LogLevel::kWarn, \"[Observer] Scan failed: \", res->failureReason);\n            break;\n        }\n    }\n\nprivate:\n    Logger &log_;\n};\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Public API (factory function)\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\nstd::unique_ptr<ScannerEngine> makeScannerEngine()\n{\n    return std::make_unique<ScannerEngine>();\n}\n\n} // namespace fl360::scanner\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// If compiled standalone, provide a trivial main() for quick smoke test\n// (Will be omitted/ignored when compiled as part of the overall suite.)\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n#ifdef FL360_SCANNER_STANDALONE\nint main()\n{\n    using namespace fl360::scanner;\n\n    auto engine = makeScannerEngine();\n    Logger l;\n    engine->registerObserver(std::make_shared<ConsoleObserver>(l));\n\n    for (int i = 0; i < 5; ++i)\n    {\n        ScanCommand cmd;\n        cmd.tenantId   = \"tenant-\" + std::to_string(i % 2);\n        cmd.resourceId = \"res-\" + std::to_string(i);\n        cmd.intensity  = (i % 2 == 0) ? ScanIntensity::kLight : ScanIntensity::kDeep;\n        engine->submit(cmd);\n    }\n\n    std::this_thread::sleep_for(std::chrono::seconds(5));\n    engine->shutdown();\n}\n#endif\n```","numLines":439,"startLine":1,"totalLines":439}}}

API Gateway - Request Entry Point\n\n```cpp\n// File: /app/project/src/services/api_gateway/routes.cpp\n// Lines: 144-149\n\nRoutes::Post(router, \"/v1/security/scan\",\n             withContext([this](const Http::Request& req,\n                                Http::ResponseWriter resp,\n                                const std::string& corrId) {\n                 this->initiateScan(req, std::move(resp), corrId);\n             }));\n```\n\n```cpp\n// File: /app/project/src/services/api_gateway/routes.cpp\n// Lines: 212-238\n\nvoid initiateScan(const Http::Request& req,\n                  Http::ResponseWriter resp,\n                  const std::string& corrId)\n{\n    const auto tenantId = ensureAuthenticated(req);\n\n    rateLimiter_.acquireToken(tenantId, \"security.scan\");\n\n    json payload = parseJson(req);\n    validators::RequestValidator::validateScanRequest(payload);\n\n    auto dispatchResult = bus_->publish(\"InitiateSecurityScan\", {\n        { \"correlationId\", corrId },\n        { \"tenantId\",      tenantId },\n        { \"scanDepth\",     payload.at(\"scanDepth\") },\n        { \"targets\",       payload.at(\"targets\") }\n    });\n\n    if (!dispatchResult) {\n        throw errors::HttpError(Http::Code::Service_Unavailable,\n                                \"Unable to queue security scan at this time\");\n    }\n\n    resp.send(Http::Code::Accepted, json{\n        { \"message\", \"Security scan queued\" },\n        { \"correlationId\", corrId }\n    }.dump());\n}\n```\n\n### 2. Command Handler - Orchestration Layer\n\n```cpp\n// File: /app/project/src/lib/orchestration/command_handler.h\n// Lines: 285-336\n\ninline CommandResult CommandHandler::Dispatch(const std::shared_ptr<ICommand>& command)\n{\n    if (!command)\n        throw std::invalid_argument(\"CommandHandler::Dispatch – command is nullptr.\");\n\n    CommandContext ctx(command);\n\n    // 1) Chain-of-Responsibility: middleware pipeline\n    for (const auto& mw : middleware_pipeline_)\n    {\n        bool proceed = false;\n        try\n        {\n            proceed = mw->Handle(ctx);\n        }\n        catch (const std::exception& ex)\n        {\n            FL360_LOG_ERROR(\"Middleware [{}] threw exception: {}\", typeid(*mw).name(), ex.what());\n            return { CommandStatus::Failed, ex.what() };\n        }\n\n        if (!proceed)\n        {\n            return { CommandStatus::Failed, \"Middleware aborted execution.\" };\n        }\n    }\n\n    // 2) Locate executor\n    std::shared_ptr<IExecutorWrapper> executor;\n    {\n        std::lock_guard lock(m_);\n        const std::type_index key(typeid(*command));\n        const auto            it = executors_.find(key);\n        if (it == executors_.end())\n        {\n            return { CommandStatus::Failed,\n                     \"No executor registered for command type: \" + std::string(key.name()) };\n        }\n        executor = it->second;\n    }\n\n    // 3) Execute\n    try\n    {\n        return executor->Execute(command, ctx);\n    }\n    catch (const std::exception& ex)\n    {\n        FL360_LOG_ERROR(\"Executor for [{}] threw exception: {}\", command->name(), ex.what());\n        return { CommandStatus::Failed, ex.what() };\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### 3. Strategy Pattern - Pay-As-You-Go vs Continuous Scan\n\n```cpp\n// File: /app/project/src/lib/domain/strategies/payg_scan_strategy.h\n// Lines: 118-182\n\nclass PayGScanStrategy final : public SecurityScanStrategy\n{\npublic:\n    /*  Tunables that can be loaded from YAML/JSON at runtime.            */\n    struct Config\n    {\n        std::chrono::milliseconds defaultTimeout  {std::chrono::seconds(30)};\n        double                    pricePerDeepScan{0.25};  // USD\n        double                    pricePerLightScan{0.10}; // USD\n        std::size_t               maxConcurrentScans {4};\n    };\n\n    // ...\n\n    std::string enqueueScan(const ScanRequest& request) override\n    {\n        /* Generate a deterministic UUID-like correlation id. */\n        const auto cid = generateCorrelationId();\n\n        {\n            std::lock_guard lock(queueMtx_);\n            if (shutdown_)\n                throw std::runtime_error(\n                        \"Cannot enqueue scan: strategy shutting down.\");\n            pending_.emplace(QueueItem{cid, request});\n        }\n        queueCv_.notify_one();\n        return cid;\n    }\n```\n\n```cpp\n// File: /app/project/src/lib/domain/strategies/continuous_scan_strategy.h\n// Lines: 107-171\n\nclass ContinuousScanStrategy final : public IScanStrategy,\n                                     public std::enable_shared_from_this<ContinuousScanStrategy>\n{\n    // ...\n\nprivate:\n    // Internal run-loop --------------------------------------------------------\n    void runLoop()\n    {\n        while (running_)\n        {\n            const auto now = std::chrono::steady_clock::now();\n            executeOneScanSafely();\n\n            std::unique_lock<std::mutex> lk(cvMutex_);\n            // Wait until either stop() is invoked or the cadence elapses.\n            cv_.wait_until(lk, now + cadence_, [this] { return !running_; });\n        }\n    }\n```\n\n### 4. Scanner Engine - THE BOTTLENECK (Single FIFO Queue)\n\n```cpp\n// File: /app/project/src/services/scanner_svc/scanner_engine.cpp\n// Lines: 198-241\n\ntemplate <typename T>\nclass BlockingQueue\n{\npublic:\n    explicit BlockingQueue(std::size_t max) : capacity_(max) {}\n\n    void push(T item)\n    {\n        std::unique_lock lk(mu_);\n        cvFull_.wait(lk, [&] { return queue_.size() < capacity_; });\n        queue_.push(std::move(item));\n        cvEmpty_.notify_one();\n    }\n\n    bool pop(T &out)\n    {\n        std::unique_lock lk(mu_);\n        cvEmpty_.wait(lk, [&] { return !queue_.empty() || terminated_; });\n        if (queue_.empty()) return false; // terminated\n        out = std::move(queue_.front());\n        queue_.pop();\n        cvFull_.notify_one();\n        return true;\n    }\n\n    void terminate()\n    {\n        {\n            std::lock_guard lg(mu_);\n            terminated_ = true;\n        }\n        cvEmpty_.notify_all();\n    }\n\nprivate:\n    std::size_t                capacity_;\n    std::queue<T>              queue_;       // <-- SIMPLE FIFO, NO PRIORITY!\n    std::mutex                 mu_;\n    std::condition_variable    cvFull_, cvEmpty_;\n    bool                       terminated_ {false};\n};\n```\n\n```cpp\n// File: /app/project/src/services/scanner_svc/scanner_engine.cpp\n// Lines: 246-296\n\nclass ScannerEngine\n{\npublic:\n    explicit ScannerEngine(std::size_t workerCount = std::thread::hardware_concurrency())\n        : queue_(1024), log_(), shuttingDown_(false)\n    {\n        workerCount = std::max<std::size_t>(1, workerCount);\n        log_.log(LogLevel::kInfo, \"Initializing ScannerEngine with \", workerCount, \" workers.\");\n\n        for (std::size_t i = 0; i < workerCount; ++i)\n        {\n            workers_.emplace_back([this, i] { workerLoop(i); });\n        }\n    }\n\n    // ...\n\n    void submit(const ScanCommand &cmd)\n    {\n        if (shuttingDown_.load()) throw std::runtime_error(\"Engine is shutting down\");\n        queue_.push(cmd);  // <-- ALL commands go to same queue, regardless of tenant type\n    }\n\nprivate:\n    void workerLoop(std::size_t idx)\n    {\n        try\n        {\n            while (true)\n            {\n                ScanCommand cmd;\n                if (!queue_.pop(cmd)) break; // queue terminated\n\n                notify(EngineEvent::kScanStarted, cmd, std::nullopt);\n\n                auto strategy = makeStrategy(cmd.intensity);  // Strategy selected AFTER dequeue\n\n                ScanResult res;\n                try\n                {\n                    res = strategy->execute(cmd);\n                    notify(EngineEvent::kScanFinished, cmd, res);\n                }\n                // ...\n            }\n        }\n        // ...\n    }\n\nprivate:\n    BlockingQueue<ScanCommand>                  queue_;  // <-- SINGLE SHARED QUEUE FOR ALL TENANTS\n    std::vector<std::thread>                    workers_;\n    // ...\n};\n```\n\n### 5. Event Flow Documentation\n\n```markdown\n// File: /app/project/docs/architecture/event_flows.md\n// Lines: 13-38\n\nsequenceDiagram\n    autonumber\n    actor Client as 🧑‍💻 Tenant / API Consumer\n    participant API as 🌐 Presentation & API\n    participant ORCH as 🔄 Orchestration\n    participant BUS as 📨 Event-Bus\n    participant SCN as 🛡️ ScannerSvc (Domain)\n    participant BCK as 💾 BackupSvc (Domain)\n    participant INF as 🛠️ Platform Adapter\n\n    Client->>API: POST /v1/scan\\n{asset: \"web-1\", tier:\"deep\"}\n    API->>ORCH: Cmd<InitiateSecurityScan>\n    ORCH->>BUS: Publish(Cmd<InitiateSecurityScan>)\n    %% Broadcast to interested domain services\n    BUS-->>SCN: Cmd<InitiateSecurityScan>\n    SCN->>SCN: Validate + Enrich\n    SCN->>BUS: Emit(Evt<SecurityScanStarted>)\n```\n\n### 6. ADR - Event-Driven Architecture Requirements\n\n```markdown\n// File: /app/project/docs/architecture/adr/002-event-driven-architecture.md\n// Lines: 15-27\n\nKey platform requirements:\n\n* **Scalability** – Burst to > 250 K events/s while maintaining < 200 ms P95 processing latency.\n* **Extensibility** – Add a new service (e.g., `ComplianceAuditor`) with zero downtime.\n* **Fault Isolation** – Failure in `BackupNode` must **not** block `AlertBroker`.\n* **Observability** – Every state transition must emit traceable, structured audit logs.\n* **Multi-tenant Guardrails** – Tenants demand isolated QoS & configurable SLAs.\n\nThese constraints steer us toward **Event-Driven Architecture (EDA)** built around an **asynchronous, durable, high-throughput event bus**.\n```\n\n## Analysis\n\n### 1. End-to-End Request Lifecycle\n\nThe on-demand security scan request follows this path:\n\n1. **API Gateway Entry** (`routes.cpp`):\n   - Client sends `POST /v1/security/scan` with tenant ID and scan parameters\n   - Request passes through authentication middleware and rate limiter\n   - Gateway publishes `InitiateSecurityScan` command to the event bus\n   - Client receives `202 Accepted` with correlation ID\n\n2. **Orchestration Layer** (`command_handler.cpp`):\n   - Command is consumed from the event bus\n   - Passes through Chain-of-Responsibility validation pipeline (TenantQuotaValidator, CompliancePolicyValidator)\n   - Strategy is selected based on scan type/tenant subscription\n   - Command is dispatched to appropriate executor\n\n3. **Event Bus Propagation** (`event_publisher.cpp`, `event_consumer.cpp`):\n   - Events are published asynchronously to NATS JetStream/Kafka\n   - Scanner service subscribes to `security.scan.requested` topic\n   - Messages are consumed with at-least-once semantics\n\n4. **Scanner Service** (`service_impl.cpp`):\n   - Receives scan command via gRPC or event bus\n   - Resolves appropriate IScanStrategy based on tenant's subscription plan\n   - Submits scan job to ScannerEngine\n\n5. **Scanner Engine Execution** (`scanner_engine.cpp`):\n   - **ALL scan commands are pushed to a single shared `BlockingQueue<ScanCommand>`**\n   - Worker threads pop commands from queue in FIFO order\n   - Strategy is instantiated and executed for each command\n\n### 2. Architectural Interaction - How Continuous Scans Affect PAYG Tenants\n\nThe interaction between tenant types occurs at multiple levels:\n\n**Strategy Level:**\n- `ContinuousScanStrategy` runs a background loop that continuously generates scan requests at a configured cadence (e.g., every 30 seconds)\n- `PayGScanStrategy` generates scans only when explicitly triggered by API calls\n\n**Scanner Engine Level (THE KEY BOTTLENECK):**\n- Both strategies ultimately submit `ScanCommand` objects to the same `ScannerEngine`\n- The `ScannerEngine` uses a **single shared FIFO queue** with capacity of 1024\n- Worker threads process commands strictly in arrival order\n\n**Timeline Example:**\n1. Continuous scan tenant \"enterprise-corp\" has 100 assets being scanned every 30 seconds\n2. PAYG tenant \"startup-inc\" requests an on-demand deep scan\n3. The PAYG request joins the queue behind 50-100 continuous scan jobs\n4. PAYG tenant must wait for all preceding jobs to complete\n\n### 3. Root Cause - Architectural Weakness\n\n**Primary Bottleneck: Lack of Quality of Service (QoS) / Priority Queue in ScannerEngine**\n\nThe `scanner_engine.cpp` implementation reveals the core issue:\n\n```cpp\nclass ScannerEngine\n{\nprivate:\n    BlockingQueue<ScanCommand> queue_;  // Single FIFO queue - no priority differentiation\n};\n```\n\nThe `BlockingQueue` is a simple FIFO structure with no awareness of:\n- **Tenant subscription tier** (PAYG vs Continuous)\n- **Request priority** (on-demand vs background)\n- **SLA requirements** (latency-sensitive vs batch processing)\n- **Tenant isolation** (no per-tenant quotas on queue utilization)\n\n**Secondary Contributing Factors:**\n\n1. **Shared Worker Pool**: All worker threads serve the same queue, with no reservation for high-priority work\n\n2. **No Request Classification**: While the `ScanPriority` enum exists in `payg_scan_strategy.h`, it's not used in the engine's queue:\n   ```cpp\n   enum class ScanPriority : std::uint8_t\n   {\n       Normal = 0,\n       High   = 1,\n   };\n   ```\n\n3. **Strategy Selection Too Late**: The scan strategy is selected *after* dequeueing, not before enqueueing - meaning the engine has no visibility into request urgency at scheduling time\n\n4. **No Back-pressure Differentiation**: When the queue fills up (capacity=1024), all incoming requests are blocked equally regardless of tenant type or urgency\n\n### 4. Why This Causes Latency for PAYG Tenants During Peak Hours\n\n1. **Volume Disparity**: Continuous scan tenants generate steady, high-volume workloads (scanning hundreds of assets on fixed intervals)\n\n2. **Queue Saturation**: During peak hours, continuous scan jobs fill the queue, pushing wait times from milliseconds to seconds or minutes\n\n3. **FIFO Unfairness**: PAYG on-demand scans, despite being latency-sensitive, wait behind batch background jobs\n\n4. **No Isolation**: There's no mechanism to ensure PAYG tenants get a fair share of compute resources\n\n5. **ADR Violation**: The documented requirement states \"Multi-tenant Guardrails – Tenants demand isolated QoS & configurable SLAs\" - this is not implemented at the scanner engine level\n\n## Implementation (For Code Modification Tasks)\n\nN/A - This is an architectural analysis task, not a code modification task.\n\n## Summary\n\n- **Primary finding**: The root cause of PAYG tenant latency during peak hours is the **lack of priority queue or QoS mechanism in the `ScannerEngine`**. All scan requests, regardless of tenant type or urgency, are processed through a single FIFO `BlockingQueue`, causing on-demand scans to wait behind high-volume continuous scan workloads.\n\n- **Key components**:\n  - `api_gateway/routes.cpp` - Request entry point\n  - `lib/orchestration/command_handler.cpp` - Command dispatching with Chain-of-Responsibility validation\n  - `lib/domain/strategies/payg_scan_strategy.h` - PAYG strategy implementation\n  - `lib/domain/strategies/continuous_scan_strategy.h` - Continuous scan strategy (high-volume producer)\n  - `lib/infrastructure/event_bus/` - Event-driven communication layer\n  - `services/scanner_svc/scanner_engine.cpp` - **THE BOTTLENECK** - single shared FIFO queue\n\n- **Architectural pattern**: The system correctly implements:\n  - Event-Driven Architecture with NATS JetStream/Kafka\n  - Command Pattern for request encapsulation\n  - Strategy Pattern for tenant-specific scan behavior\n  - Chain-of-Responsibility for validation pipeline\n  - Observer Pattern for metrics and audit logging\n\n  However, it **fails to implement** proper QoS/priority scheduling at the worker level.\n\n- **Recommendations**:\n  1. Replace `BlockingQueue<ScanCommand>` with a **priority queue** that respects `ScanPriority` and tenant subscription tier\n  2. Implement **separate queues** for on-demand (low-latency) vs. background (batch) workloads\n  3. Reserve a portion of worker threads for high-priority work\n  4. Add **per-tenant quotas** to prevent any single tenant from monopolizing the queue\n  5. Implement **weighted fair queuing** to ensure PAYG tenants get guaranteed compute share\n  6. Consider using **separate worker pools** for different tenant tiers (as suggested by the `maxConcurrentScans` config in `PayGScanStrategy::Config`)\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":359,"cache_read_input_tokens":122086,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":359,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":24,"service_tier":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"f14367f7-503f-4acf-8fd0-3a7f9ede3bf3","uuid":"d8907c51-8b06-4138-bf54-521b1c8a3f4e"}

 // <-- SINGLE SHARED QUEUE FOR ALL TENANTS\n    std::vector<std::thread>                    workers_;\n    // ...\n};\n```\n\n### 5. Event Flow Documentation\n\n```markdown\n// File: /app/project/docs/architecture/event_flows.md\n// Lines: 13-38\n\nsequenceDiagram\n    autonumber\n    actor Client as 🧑‍💻 Tenant / API Consumer\n    participant API as 🌐 Presentation & API\n    participant ORCH as 🔄 Orchestration\n    participant BUS as 📨 Event-Bus\n    participant SCN as 🛡️ ScannerSvc (Domain)\n    participant BCK as 💾 BackupSvc (Domain)\n    participant INF as 🛠️ Platform Adapter\n\n    Client->>API: POST /v1/scan\\n{asset: \"web-1\", tier:\"deep\"}\n    API->>ORCH: Cmd<InitiateSecurityScan>\n    ORCH->>BUS: Publish(Cmd<InitiateSecurityScan>)\n    %% Broadcast to interested domain services\n    BUS-->>SCN: Cmd<InitiateSecurityScan>\n    SCN->>SCN: Validate + Enrich\n    SCN->>BUS: Emit(Evt<SecurityScanStarted>)\n```\n\n### 6. ADR - Event-Driven Architecture Requirements\n\n```markdown\n// File: /app/project/docs/architecture/adr/002-event-driven-architecture.md\n// Lines: 15-27\n\nKey platform requirements:\n\n* **Scalability** – Burst to > 250 K events/s while maintaining < 200 ms P95 processing latency.\n* **Extensibility** – Add a new service (e.g., `ComplianceAuditor`) with zero downtime.\n* **Fault Isolation** – Failure in `BackupNode` must **not** block `AlertBroker`.\n* **Observability** – Every state transition must emit traceable, structured audit logs.\n* **Multi-tenant Guardrails** – Tenants demand isolated QoS & configurable SLAs.\n\nThese constraints steer us toward **Event-Driven Architecture (EDA)** built around an **asynchronous, durable, high-throughput event bus**.\n```\n\n## Analysis\n\n### 1. End-to-End Request Lifecycle\n\nThe on-demand security scan request follows this path:\n\n1. **API Gateway Entry** (`routes.cpp`):\n   - Client sends `POST /v1/security/scan` with tenant ID and scan parameters\n   - Request passes through authentication middleware and rate limiter\n   - Gateway publishes `InitiateSecurityScan` command to the event bus\n   - Client receives `202 Accepted` with correlation ID\n\n2. **Orchestration Layer** (`command_handler.cpp`):\n   - Command is consumed from the event bus\n   - Passes through Chain-of-Responsibility validation pipeline (TenantQuotaValidator, CompliancePolicyValidator)\n   - Strategy is selected based on scan type/tenant subscription\n   - Command is dispatched to appropriate executor\n\n3. **Event Bus Propagation** (`event_publisher.cpp`, `event_consumer.cpp`):\n   - Events are published asynchronously to NATS JetStream/Kafka\n   - Scanner service subscribes to `security.scan.requested` topic\n   - Messages are consumed with at-least-once semantics\n\n4. **Scanner Service** (`service_impl.cpp`):\n   - Receives scan command via gRPC or event bus\n   - Resolves appropriate IScanStrategy based on tenant's subscription plan\n   - Submits scan job to ScannerEngine\n\n5. **Scanner Engine Execution** (`scanner_engine.cpp`):\n   - **ALL scan commands are pushed to a single shared `BlockingQueue<ScanCommand>`**\n   - Worker threads pop commands from queue in FIFO order\n   - Strategy is instantiated and executed for each command\n\n### 2. Architectural Interaction - How Continuous Scans Affect PAYG Tenants\n\nThe interaction between tenant types occurs at multiple levels:\n\n**Strategy Level:**\n- `ContinuousScanStrategy` runs a background loop that continuously generates scan requests at a configured cadence (e.g., every 30 seconds)\n- `PayGScanStrategy` generates scans only when explicitly triggered by API calls\n\n**Scanner Engine Level (THE KEY BOTTLENECK):**\n- Both strategies ultimately submit `ScanCommand` objects to the same `ScannerEngine`\n- The `ScannerEngine` uses a **single shared FIFO queue** with capacity of 1024\n- Worker threads process commands strictly in arrival order\n\n**Timeline Example:**\n1. Continuous scan tenant \"enterprise-corp\" has 100 assets being scanned every 30 seconds\n2. PAYG tenant \"startup-inc\" requests an on-demand deep scan\n3. The PAYG request joins the queue behind 50-100 continuous scan jobs\n4. PAYG tenant must wait for all preceding jobs to complete\n\n### 3. Root Cause - Architectural Weakness\n\n**Primary Bottleneck: Lack of Quality of Service (QoS) / Priority Queue in ScannerEngine**\n\nThe `scanner_engine.cpp` implementation reveals the core issue:\n\n```cpp\nclass ScannerEngine\n{\nprivate:\n    BlockingQueue<ScanCommand> queue_;  // Single FIFO queue - no priority differentiation\n};\n```\n\nThe `BlockingQueue` is a simple FIFO structure with no awareness of:\n- **Tenant subscription tier** (PAYG vs Continuous)\n- **Request priority** (on-demand vs background)\n- **SLA requirements** (latency-sensitive vs batch processing)\n- **Tenant isolation** (no per-tenant quotas on queue utilization)\n\n**Secondary Contributing Factors:**\n\n1. **Shared Worker Pool**: All worker threads serve the same queue, with no reservation for high-priority work\n\n2. **No Request Classification**: While the `ScanPriority` enum exists in `payg_scan_strategy.h`, it's not used in the engine's queue:\n   ```cpp\n   enum class ScanPriority : std::uint8_t\n   {\n       Normal = 0,\n       High   = 1,\n   };\n   ```\n\n3. **Strategy Selection Too Late**: The scan strategy is selected *after* dequeueing, not before enqueueing - meaning the engine has no visibility into request urgency at scheduling time\n\n4. **No Back-pressure Differentiation**: When the queue fills up (capacity=1024), all incoming requests are blocked equally regardless of tenant type or urgency\n\n### 4. Why This Causes Latency for PAYG Tenants During Peak Hours\n\n1. **Volume Disparity**: Continuous scan tenants generate steady, high-volume workloads (scanning hundreds of assets on fixed intervals)\n\n2. **Queue Saturation**: During peak hours, continuous scan jobs fill the queue, pushing wait times from milliseconds to seconds or minutes\n\n3. **FIFO Unfairness**: PAYG on-demand scans, despite being latency-sensitive, wait behind batch background jobs\n\n4. **No Isolation**: There's no mechanism to ensure PAYG tenants get a fair share of compute resources\n\n5. **ADR Violation**: The documented requirement states \"Multi-tenant Guardrails – Tenants demand isolated QoS & configurable SLAs\" - this is not implemented at the scanner engine level\n\n## Implementation (For Code Modification Tasks)\n\nN/A - This is an architectural analysis task, not a code modification task.\n\n## Summary\n\n- **Primary finding**: The root cause of PAYG tenant latency during peak hours is the **lack of priority queue or QoS mechanism in the `ScannerEngine`**. All scan requests, regardless of tenant type or urgency, are processed through a single FIFO `BlockingQueue`, causing on-demand scans to wait behind high-volume continuous scan workloads.\n\n- **Key components**:\n  - `api_gateway/routes.cpp` - Request entry point\n  - `lib/orchestration/command_handler.cpp` - Command dispatching with Chain-of-Responsibility validation\n  - `lib/domain/strategies/payg_scan_strategy.h` - PAYG strategy implementation\n  - `lib/domain/strategies/continuous_scan_strategy.h` - Continuous scan strategy (high-volume producer)\n  - `lib/infrastructure/event_bus/` - Event-driven communication layer\n  - `services/scanner_svc/scanner_engine.cpp` - **THE BOTTLENECK** - single shared FIFO queue\n\n- **Architectural pattern**: The system correctly implements:\n  - Event-Driven Architecture with NATS JetStream/Kafka\n  - Command Pattern for request encapsulation\n  - Strategy Pattern for tenant-specific scan behavior\n  - Chain-of-Responsibility for validation pipeline\n  - Observer Pattern for metrics and audit logging\n\n  However, it **fails to implement** proper QoS/priority scheduling at the worker level.\n\n- **Recommendations**:\n  1. Replace `BlockingQueue<ScanCommand>` with a **priority queue** that respects `ScanPriority` and tenant subscription tier\n  2. Implement **separate queues** for on-demand (low-latency) vs. background (batch) workloads\n  3. Reserve a portion of worker threads for high-priority work\n  4. Add **per-tenant quotas** to prevent any single tenant from monopolizing the queue\n  5. Implement **weighted fair queuing** to ensure PAYG tenants get guaranteed compute share\n  6. Consider using **separate worker pools** for different tenant tiers (as suggested by the `maxConcurrentScans` config in `PayGScanStrategy::Config`)\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
